<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We resolve the contact-Fano homogeneity conjecture and the Riemannian LeBrun–Salamon conjecture positively. Every smooth connected complex projective contact Fano manifold of complex dimension at least three, with its given contact distribution, is contact-isomorphic to the adjoint variety of a simple complex Lie algebra. Consequently, every closed connected smooth positive quaternionic-Kähler manifold of real dimension $`4m\geq8`$ is homothetic to a compact symmetric Wolf space.

- **C1** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $(X,F)$ be a smooth connected complex projective contact Fano manifold of dimension $2n+1$, with $n\geq1$, and let $L=T_X/F$. There exist a finite-dimensional simple complex Lie algebra $\mathfrak g$ and an algebraic isomorphism $$\varphi:X\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }Y_{\mathfrak g}$$ whose differential carries the given distribution $F$ to the canonical contact distribution on $Y_{\mathfrak g}$. Moreover, $$L\simeq\varphi^*\bigl(\mathcal O_{\mathbb P(\mathfrak g)}(1)\|_{Y_{\mathfrak g}}\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Classifies the given smooth projective contact Fano structure as the canonical structure on a simple adjoint variety, preserving its contact line bundle.
- **C2** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-contact-fano-ke)): Let $(X,F)$ be a smooth connected complex projective contact Fano manifold of complex dimension $2n+1$, where $n\geq1$. Then $X$ admits a Kähler–Einstein metric of positive scalar curvature for its given complex structure.
  - Explanation: Establishes positive Kähler–Einstein existence for the given complex structure under the contact Fano hypotheses.
- **C3** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-projective-contact)): Let $(X,F)$ be a smooth connected complex projective contact manifold of dimension $2n+1$, where $n\geq1$. Its second Betti number determines the following alternatives.* 1. *If $b_2(X)=1$, then $(X,F)$ is contact-isomorphic to the adjoint variety $Y_{\mathfrak g}$, with its canonical contact distribution, for a simple complex Lie algebra $\mathfrak g$.* 2. *If $b_2(X)\geq2$, then there is a smooth connected complex projective manifold $Z$ of dimension $n+1$ and a biholomorphism $$X\simeq\mathbb P(T^*Z).$$ Here $\mathbb P(T^*Z)$ parametrizes lines in cotangent spaces, equivalently hyperplanes in tangent spaces. This assertion identifies the underlying manifolds, without imposing compatibility with the given distribution $F$.* *Conversely, every adjoint variety of complex dimension $2n+1$ and every projectivized cotangent bundle in the second alternative admits a contact structure. Thus these are precisely the underlying manifolds of smooth connected complex projective contact manifolds in these dimensions.
  - Explanation: Separates projective contact manifolds by their second Betti number; the cotangent alternative identifies underlying manifolds without requiring compatibility with the given distribution.
- **C4** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-quaternionic)): Let $(M^{4m},g)$ be a closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold, where $m\geq2$, with holonomy contained in $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(m)\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(1)$ in the standard representation and with positive scalar curvature. Then $$\nabla\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}_g=0.$$ Moreover, $(M,g)$ is homothetic to a compact symmetric positive quaternionic-Kähler Wolf space.
  - Explanation: Under the stated closed positive quaternionic-Kähler hypotheses, the metric has parallel curvature and is homothetic to a symmetric Wolf space.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-red-simply-connected)): The manifold $X$ is simply connected, and its Picard group is a free abelian group of finite rank, canonically isomorphic to $H^2(X,\mathbb Z)$.
  - Explanation: Gives simple connectivity and the torsion-free cohomological description of the Picard group in the contact Fano setting.
- **C6** (classification, [source](#stmt-red-alternatives)): Either $\operatorname{Pic}(X)=\mathbb Z[L]$, or $(X,F)$ is contact-isomorphic to one of the following standard contact manifolds:* 1. *the incidence variety $$I=\{(\ell,H)\in\mathbb P^{n+1}\times(\mathbb P^{n+1})^*: \ell\subset H\}, \qquad L=\mathcal O_I(1,1);$$* 2. *projective space $\mathbb P^{2n+1}$ with the contact structure induced by a nondegenerate alternating form on $\mathbb C^{2n+2}$, and $L=\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^{2n+1}}(2)$.* *In both listed cases a connected algebraic group acts transitively and preserves the given distribution $F$.
  - Explanation: Separates the primitive Picard regime from the incidence and symplectic projective models, including their given contact distributions.
- **C7** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lines-fixed-degree)): Let $A$ be an ample line bundle on a smooth projective variety $M$, and fix an integer $e>0$. There is a dense open $M_e\subset M$ such that every map $f:\mathbb P^1\to M$ of $A$-degree $e$ whose image meets $M_e$ is free, that is, $f^*T_M$ is globally generated.
  - Explanation: For a fixed ample degree, maps meeting the specified dense open locus have globally generated pulled-back tangent bundles.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-lines-positive)): For a free contact line $f$, let $P_f\subset f^*T_X$ be the positive part of its splitting. This subbundle is intrinsic, the quotient $f^*T_X/P_f$ is trivial, and $$\label{lines:positive-horizontal} \theta(P_f)=0.$$ Moreover $P_f$ is isotropic for the pulled-back Levi form, so $\operatorname{rank}P_f\leq n$.
  - Explanation: The positive splitting part is intrinsic, horizontal and isotropic, with trivial quotient and the stated rank bound.
- **C9** (construction, [source](#stmt-lines-free-cover)): There is a smooth irreducible family $H$ of parametrized free contact lines with smooth dominant evaluation $H\times\mathbb P^1\to X$.
  - Explanation: Provides a smooth covering family of free parametrized contact lines with smooth dominant evaluation.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lines-cone-addition)): Let $(V,\omega)$ be a symplectic vector space of dimension $2n$. Let $Z\subset\mathbb P(V)$ be a smooth projective Legendrian variety, possibly disconnected. Write $U_i$ for the affine cones over its irreducible components. If $\omega(a,b)\neq0$ for some $a\in U_i$, $b\in U_j$, then the addition map $$U_i\times U_j\longrightarrow V,\qquad (a,b)\longmapsto a+b$$ is dominant.
  - Explanation: Nonorthogonal points on smooth Legendrian cone components force dominance of their addition map, allowing disconnected direction varieties.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-lines-suitable-pair)): There exist a point $x\in X$ and free parametrized contact lines $f_1,f_2:\mathbb P^1\to X$, with $f_1(0)=f_2(0)=x$, such that, for affine parameters at $0$ and $a=f'_1(0)$, $b=f'_2(0)$, $$\label{lines:pair} d\alpha_x(a,b)\neq0,\qquad P_{f_1}\|_0+P_{f_2}\|_0=F_x.$$ In particular the two positive subspaces are complementary Lagrangian subspaces of $F_x$.
  - Explanation: Produces free contact lines with nonzero Levi pairing and complementary positive Lagrangian subspaces.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sm-relative-hom)): Let $S$ be a smooth irreducible complex curve, let $\mathscr Y\to S$ be a projective flat family of nodal curves, and let $Z$ be a smooth projective variety. The relative scheme of maps $$\operatorname{Hom}_S(\mathscr Y,Z\times S)\longrightarrow S$$ is locally of finite type. At a map $f:Y_{s_0}\to Z$ its relative tangent space is $H^0(Y_{s_0},f^*T_Z)$. If $H^1(Y_{s_0},f^*T_Z)=0$, the displayed morphism is smooth at $[f]$. In particular its total space is smooth there and its differential onto $T_{s_0}S$ is surjective.
  - Explanation: Vanishing of the pulled-back tangent obstruction group yields smoothness of the full relative map scheme over a nodal source family.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sm-vertical-cokernel)): The cokernel of evaluation on the vertical tangent space is canonically $$\label{sm:cokernel} \mathop{\mathrm{coker}}\bigl[ H^0(C,E)\longrightarrow T_{y_1}X\oplus T_{y_2}X \bigr] \simeq H^1(C,E(-p-q)) \simeq T_xX/F_x.$$ In particular it has dimension one.
  - Explanation: Identifies the vertical evaluation obstruction with the contact quotient at the node.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sm-fixed-ends)): There is no tangent vector $\zeta\in T_{[f_0]}\mathscr H$ such that $$d\sigma(\zeta)=1,\qquad d\operatorname{Ev}(\zeta)=0.$$
  - Explanation: Excludes an infinitesimal smoothing direction that fixes both endpoint values for the selected contact-line pair.
- **C15** (construction, [source](#stmt-sm-very-free)): There is a map $g:\mathbb P^1\to X$ with $\deg g^*L=2$ and $$\label{sm:positive-splitting} g^*T_X\simeq\mathcal O(2)\oplus\mathcal O(1)^{\oplus2n}.$$ The full scheme $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_2(\mathbb P^1,X)$ of degree-two maps is smooth at $g$, and evaluation at two fixed distinct parameters is submersive there. Its irreducible component through $g$ therefore dominates $X\times X$ by two-point evaluation. Moreover $g$ is an immersion, and strict positivity of the tangent splitting persists on an open neighborhood of $g$.
  - Explanation: Constructs a very free contact conic with the stated tangent splitting and retains its full smooth deformation neighborhood.
- **C16** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-sm-full-germ)): Let $\beta$ be the numerical curve class of $g$, specified by its degree functional on $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)$. The stack $\overline{\mathcal M}_{0,0}(X,\beta)$ is smooth at $[g]$, and has a unique irreducible component through that point. If $\mathcal S$ is the normalization of that reduced component, it agrees with the original stack on an open neighborhood of $[g]$. After shrinking an open neighborhood $\mathcal H$ of $g$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_2(\mathbb P^1,X)$, the map forgetting parametrization factors through $\mathcal S$, and the universal stable-map family pulls back to the full family $\mathcal H\times\mathbb P^1\to X$.
  - Explanation: Transfers the full parametrized neighborhood to the normalized component of the unmarked stable-map stack without losing variations.
- **C17** (construction, [source](#stmt-ext-moving-pair)): There are a proper integral algebraic space $Y$, a dominant morphism $p:Y\to X\times X$, and sections $$W_{12}\in H^0(Y,p^*\mathcal E_{12}),\qquad W_{21}\in H^0(Y,p^*\mathcal E_{21})$$ with common contact value $$\xi=(\theta\boxtimes 1)(W_{12}) =(1\boxtimes\theta)(W_{21}) \in H^0(Y,p^*\mathcal M_X).$$ There is an involution $\iota$ of $Y$ over the interchange of the two factors of $X\times X$, under which $\xi$ is invariant using the canonical interchange of the line factors.* *Moreover, for a full open neighborhood $\mathcal H$ of $g$ in $\operatorname{Hom}_2(\mathbb P^1,X)$ there is a morphism $$j:\mathcal H\times\mathbb P^1\times\mathbb P^1\longrightarrow Y, \qquad p(j(h,z,w))=(h(z),h(w)).$$ In an affine coordinate containing $z,w$, its pullbacks satisfy $$\label{ext:hom-vectors} \begin{aligned} j^*W_{12}(h,z,w) &=(z-w)^2h'(z)\otimes\theta(h'(w)),\\ j^*W_{21}(h,z,w) &=(z-w)^2\theta(h'(z))\otimes h'(w), \end{aligned}$$ and hence $$\label{ext:hom-identity} j^*\xi(h,z,w) =(z-w)^2\theta(h'(z))\otimes\theta(h'(w)).$$ These formulas are independent of the coordinate.
  - Explanation: Extends both vector lifts to a proper moving-pair space, retaining the common contact value, interchange symmetry and full local family identities.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ext-normal-products)): Let $B$ be a normal integral locally Noetherian scheme, and let $C_i\to B$, for $1\leq i\leq k$, be flat nodal-curve families of finite presentation. Suppose the *entire* generic fiber of each family is smooth and geometrically integral. Then $P=C_1\times_B\cdots\times_B C_k$ is integral and normal. Every height-one point of $P$ lies either over the generic point of $B$, or at a generic point of a fiber component over a height-one point of $B$. In the latter case every selected point of every $C_i$ is relatively smooth.
  - Explanation: Flat nodal products over a normal base are integral and normal when the entire generic fibers are smooth and geometrically integral; divisorial points select smooth source points.
- **C19** (classification, [source](#stmt-ext-trees)): A geometric source of an unmarked stable map of genus zero and $L$-degree two is either smooth, or is exactly two copies $A,J$ of $\mathbb P^1$, joined at one node, with $L$-degree one on each. There are no contracted components.
  - Explanation: Unmarked stable maps of the specified contact degree have either smooth source or a pair of noncontracted rational components.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-ext-contact-order)): On the relatively smooth locus at the generic point of either $A$ or $J$, the bundle map $\theta d_\pi e:T_{C/R}\to e^*L$ is divisible by $\tau^r$.
  - Explanation: The contact differential vanishes at least to the node thickness along either special component at its relatively smooth generic point.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-ext-differential-order)): With the notation above, $b_J=r$.
  - Explanation: The contraction differential has component order equal to the node thickness.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-des-descent)): Let $X$ be a smooth simply connected complex projective contact Fano manifold with $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)=\mathbb Z[L]$. Assume that a family of free contact lines covers a dense open subset of $X$. Let $p:Y\to Z$ be dominant, where $Y$ is a proper integral algebraic space over $\mathbb C$. Suppose that there are regular sections $$W_{12}\in H^0\bigl(Y,p^*(T_X\boxtimes L)\bigr),\qquad W_{21}\in H^0\bigl(Y,p^*(L\boxtimes T_X)\bigr)$$ with the same contact value $$\label{des:common-value} \xi=(\theta\boxtimes 1)(W_{12}) =(1\boxtimes\theta)(W_{21})\in H^0(Y,p^*M).$$ Then there is a unique section $B\in H^0(Z,M)$ such that $p^*B=\xi$.
  - Explanation: The proper family contact value descends uniquely when both vector lifts, primitive Picard structure, simple connectivity and covering free lines are available.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-des-family)): Apply Proposition <a href="#des:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="des:descent">22</a> to the space and tensors of Proposition <a href="#ext:moving-pair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:moving-pair">17</a>. The resulting section $B\in H^0(X\times X,L\boxtimes L)$ is symmetric under exchange of the factors, using the canonical exchange of the two line factors. For the full neighborhood $\mathcal H$ of $g$ in the space of parametrized degree-two maps provided there, one has $$\label{des:family-identity} B\bigl(h(z),h(w)\bigr) =(z-w)^2\,\theta\bigl(dh(\partial_z)\bigr) \otimes\theta\bigl(dh(\partial_w)\bigr), \qquad h\in\mathcal H.$$ This identity holds for all pairs of source points, including the diagonal; its displayed expression uses any common affine coordinate.
  - Explanation: The descended contact value is symmetric and satisfies the full moving-point identity on the full parametrized neighborhood.
- **C24** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-value-velocity)): Fix the parameter $0\in\mathbb P^1$ and its affine coordinate. There is a Zariski-open neighborhood $\mathcal U\subset\mathcal H$ of $g$ on which the value–velocity morphism $$\mathcal U\longrightarrow T_X, \qquad h\longmapsto\bigl(h(0),h'(0)\bigr),$$ is smooth. Its image is open in $T_X$. Its projection is a nonempty open subset $U\subset X$, and for each $x\in U$ the velocities of maps in $\mathcal U$ with $h(0)=x$ form a nonempty open subset of $T_xX$.
  - Explanation: Positive tangent splitting supplies a smooth value–velocity map and open sets of velocities over an open set of target points.
- **C25** (bound, [source](#stmt-jet-diagonal)): The section $B$ vanishes to order at least two along $\Delta$, and its second diagonal symbol is $$\label{jet:second-symbol} B\in H^0(X\times X,\mathcal I^2\otimes M), \qquad [B]_2=\theta^2 \quad\text{in }H^0(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\Omega_X^1\otimes L^{\otimes2}).$$
  - Explanation: The full local family determines the global quadratic diagonal symbol after both lower symbols vanish.
- **C26** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-local-calculation)): Let $B$ be symmetric and satisfy Equation <a href="#jet:second-symbol" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="jet:second-symbol">[jet:second-symbol]</a> on a complex contact manifold. In local coordinates and a frame as above, put $s=y-x$ and $A_x(u,v)=(\partial_u\alpha)_x(v)$, where $\partial$ differentiates the coefficients of $\alpha$. Then $$\label{jet:cubic} b(x,x+s)=\alpha_x(s)^2+\alpha_x(s)A_x(s,s)+O(s^4).$$ Differentiation in the first slot, with the second slot held fixed, gives $$\label{jet:fixed-second} \begin{split} D^{(1)}_v b(x,x+s) ={}&-2\alpha_x(s)\alpha_x(v) +\alpha_x(s)d\alpha_x(v,s)\\ &\hspace{8mm}-\alpha_x(v)A_x(s,s)+O(s^3). \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: Symmetry fixes the cubic local Taylor term and its fixed-second-slot derivative, exposing the Levi form.
- **C27** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-hamiltonian)): On a complex contact manifold, contact value identifies the holomorphic vector fields preserving $F$ with $H^0(X,L)$ [[LeBrun1995](#ref-LeBrun1995), Proposition 2.1]. For $a\in H^0(X,L)$, write $K_a$ for the corresponding field. In a frame $\theta=e\alpha$, $a=e f$, it is characterized by $$\label{jet:hamiltonian-equations} \alpha(K_a)=f, \qquad df(w)+d\alpha(K_a,w)=0\quad(w\in F).$$ Its value at a point depends only on the ordinary first jet of $a$.
  - Explanation: Global sections of the contact line are contact Hamiltonians; their ordinary first jets determine the corresponding field values.
- **C28** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-criterion)): Let $X$ be a connected compact complex contact manifold with contact quotient $\theta:T_X\to L$, and let $\mathcal I_\Delta$ be the ideal sheaf of the diagonal in $X\times X$. Suppose that a symmetric section $B\in H^0(X\times X,L\boxtimes L)$ satisfies $$\begin{gathered} B\in H^0(X\times X,\mathcal I_\Delta^2\otimes(L\boxtimes L)),\\ [B]_2=\theta^2 \quad\text{in }H^0(X,\mathop{\mathrm{Sym}}^2\Omega_X^1\otimes L^{\otimes2}). \end{gathered}$$ Then the ordinary first-jet evaluation map $$\label{jet:jet-generation} H^0(X,L)\longrightarrow J_x^1L$$ is surjective at every $x\in X$. Global contact vector fields span $T_xX$ at every point, and the identity component of the holomorphic contact automorphism group acts transitively.
  - Explanation: A symmetric tensor with the prescribed quadratic diagonal symbol generates ordinary first jets and yields contact transitivity on a connected compact complex contact manifold.
- **C29** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-jet-transitive)): For every smooth contact Fano manifold in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the identity component $G=\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(X,F)^0$ is a connected linear algebraic group acting transitively on $X$.
  - Explanation: Contact transitivity in the Fano setting is realized by a connected linear algebraic automorphism group.
- **C30** (classification, [source](#stmt-hom-adjoint)): Let $(X,F)$ be a smooth connected complex projective contact manifold with ample contact line $L$. Suppose a connected algebraic group $G$ acts effectively and transitively on $X$, preserving $F$. Then $\mathfrak g=\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}(G)$ is simple, and contact values of fundamental vector fields define an isomorphism $$\Phi:(X,F)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \bigl(\mathbb P(\mathcal O_{\min}(\mathfrak g)),F_{\mathrm{can}}\bigr).$$ Under this isomorphism, $L$ is the restriction of the hyperplane line bundle of $\mathbb P(\mathfrak g)$.
  - Explanation: An effective transitive algebraic contact action with ample contact line identifies the manifold and its distribution with a simple adjoint contact variety.
- **C31** (classification, [source](#stmt-hom-models)): The incidence and projective-space alternatives in Proposition <a href="#red:alternatives" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="red:alternatives">6</a> are the adjoint contact varieties of $\mathfrak{sl}_{n+2}(\mathbb C)$ and $\mathfrak{sp}_{2n+2}(\mathbb C)$, respectively. Their adjoint polarizations are $\mathcal O(1,1)$ and $\mathcal O(2)$.
  - Explanation: Identifies the incidence and symplectic projective alternatives with their adjoint polarizations.
- **C32** (construction, [source](#stmt-jet-good-curves)): In the primitive Picard case, every point of $X$ lies on an immersed rational curve $u:\mathbb P^1\to X$ satisfying $$u^*L\simeq\mathcal O(2),\qquad \theta\,du:T_{\mathbb P^1}\xrightarrow{\sim}u^*L, \qquad u^*F\simeq\mathcal O(1)^{\oplus2n}.$$
  - Explanation: In the primitive Picard regime, immersed transverse contact conics with the stated contact-bundle splitting pass through every point.

        "anchor": "stmt-sm-relative-hom",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S$ be a smooth irreducible complex curve, let $\\mathscr Y\\to S$ be a projective flat family of nodal curves, and let $Z$ be a smooth projective variety. The relative scheme of maps $$\\operatorname{Hom}_S(\\mathscr Y,Z\\times S)\\longrightarrow S$$ is locally of finite type. At a map $f:Y_{s_0}\\to Z$ its relative tangent space is $H^0(Y_{s_0},f^*T_Z)$. If $H^1(Y_{s_0},f^*T_Z)=0$, the displayed morphism is smooth at $[f]$. In particular its total space is smooth there and its differential onto $T_{s_0}S$ is surjective.",
        "source_id": "PM-062.1"

        "anchor": "stmt-sm-very-free",
        "exact_quote": "There is a map $g:\\mathbb P^1\\to X$ with $\\deg g^*L=2$ and $$\\label{sm:positive-splitting}\ng^*T_X\\simeq\\mathcal O(2)\\oplus\\mathcal O(1)^{\\oplus2n}.$$ The full scheme $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_2(\\mathbb P^1,X)$ of degree-two maps is smooth at $g$, and evaluation at two fixed distinct parameters is submersive there. Its irreducible component through $g$ therefore dominates $X\\times X$ by two-point evaluation.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sm-full-germ",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\beta$ be the numerical curve class of $g$, specified by its degree functional on $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(X)$. The stack $\\overline{\\mathcal M}_{0,0}(X,\\beta)$ is smooth at $[g]$, and has a unique irreducible component through that point. If $\\mathcal S$ is the normalization of that reduced component, it agrees with the original stack on an open neighborhood of $[g]$. After shrinking an open neighborhood $\\mathcal H$ of $g$ in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_2(\\mathbb P^1,X)$, the map forgetting parametrization factors through $\\mathcal S$, and the universal stable-map family pulls back to the full family $\\mathcal H\\times\\mathbb P^1\\to X$.",
        "source_id": "PM-062.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sm-full-germ",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ext-moving-pair",
        "exact_quote": "There are a proper integral algebraic space $Y$, a dominant morphism $p:Y\\to X\\times X$, and sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{12}),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{21})$$ with common contact value $$\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n     =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\n\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal M_X).$$ There is an involution $\\iota$ of $Y$ over the interchange of the two factors of $X\\times X$, under which $\\xi$ is invariant using the canonical interchange of the line factors.*\n\n\n\n*Moreover, for a full open neighborhood $\\mathcal H$ of $g$ in $\\operatorname{Hom}_2(\\mathbb P^1,X)$ there is a morphism $$j:\\mathcal H\\times\\mathbb P^1\\times\\mathbb P^1\\longrightarrow Y,\n\\qquad p(j(h,z,w))=(h(z),h(w)).$$ In an affine coordinate containing $z,w$, its pullbacks satisfy $$\\label{ext:hom-vectors}\n\\begin{aligned}\nj^*W_{12}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2h'(z)\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)),\\\\\nj^*W_{21}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes h'(w),\n\\end{aligned}$$ and hence $$\\label{ext:hom-identity}\nj^*\\xi(h,z,w)\n  =(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)).$$ These formulas are independent of the coordinate.",
        "source_id": "PM-062.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ext-moving-pair",
          "exact_quote": "There are a proper integral algebraic space $Y$, a dominant morphism $p:Y\\to X\\times X$, and sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{12}),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{21})$$ with common contact value $$\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n     =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\n\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal M_X).$$ There is an involution $\\iota$ of $Y$ over the interchange of the two factors of $X\\times X$, under which $\\xi$ is invariant using the canonical interchange of the line factors.*\n\n\n\n*Moreover, for a full open neighborhood $\\mathcal H$ of $g$ in $\\operatorname{Hom}_2(\\mathbb P^1,X)$ there is a morphism $$j:\\mathcal H\\times\\mathbb P^1\\times\\mathbb P^1\\longrightarrow Y,\n\\qquad p(j(h,z,w))=(h(z),h(w)).$$ In an affine coordinate containing $z,w$, its pullbacks satisfy $$\\label{ext:hom-vectors}\n\\begin{aligned}\nj^*W_{12}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2h'(z)\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)),\\\\\nj^*W_{21}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes h'(w),\n\\end{aligned}$$ and hence $$\\label{ext:hom-identity}\nj^*\\xi(h,z,w)\n  =(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)).$$ These formulas are independent of the coordinate.",
          "source_id": "PM-062.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are a proper integral algebraic space $Y$, a dominant morphism $p:Y\\to X\\times X$, and sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{12}),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{21})$$ with common contact value $$\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n     =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\n\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal M_X).$$ There is an involution $\\iota$ of $Y$ over the interchange of the two factors of $X\\times X$, under which $\\xi$ is invariant using the canonical interchange of the line factors.*\n\n\n\n*Moreover, for a full open neighborhood $\\mathcal H$ of $g$ in $\\operatorname{Hom}_2(\\mathbb P^1,X)$ there is a morphism $$j:\\mathcal H\\times\\mathbb P^1\\times\\mathbb P^1\\longrightarrow Y,\n\\qquad p(j(h,z,w))=(h(z),h(w)).$$ In an affine coordinate containing $z,w$, its pullbacks satisfy $$\\label{ext:hom-vectors}\n\\begin{aligned}\nj^*W_{12}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2h'(z)\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)),\\\\\nj^*W_{21}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes h'(w),\n\\end{aligned}$$ and hence $$\\label{ext:hom-identity}\nj^*\\xi(h,z,w)\n  =(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)).$$ These formulas are independent of the coordinate.",
      "explanation": "Extends both vector lifts to a proper moving-pair space, retaining the common contact value, interchange symmetry and full local family identities.",

      "hypotheses": [

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ext-normal-products",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a normal integral locally Noetherian scheme, and let $C_i\\to B$, for $1\\leq i\\leq k$, be flat nodal-curve families of finite presentation. Suppose the *entire* generic fiber of each family is smooth and geometrically integral. Then $P=C_1\\times_B\\cdots\\times_B C_k$ is integral and normal. Every height-one point of $P$ lies either over the generic point of $B$, or at a generic point of a fiber component over a height-one point of $B$. In the latter case every selected point of every $C_i$ is relatively smooth.",
        "source_id": "PM-062.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ext-normal-products",

Every height-one point of $P$ lies either over the generic point of $B$, or at a generic point of a fiber component over a height-one point of $B$. In the latter case every selected point of every $C_i$ is relatively smooth.",
          "source_id": "PM-062.1"
        }
      ],

In the latter case every selected point of every $C_i$ is relatively smooth.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-des-descent",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth simply connected complex projective contact Fano manifold with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(X)=\\mathbb Z[L]$. Assume that a family of free contact lines covers a dense open subset of $X$. Let $p:Y\\to Z$ be dominant, where $Y$ is a proper integral algebraic space over $\\mathbb C$. Suppose that there are regular sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(T_X\\boxtimes L)\\bigr),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(L\\boxtimes T_X)\\bigr)$$ with the same contact value $$\\label{des:common-value}\n\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n    =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\\in H^0(Y,p^*M).$$ Then there is a unique section $B\\in H^0(Z,M)$ such that $p^*B=\\xi$.",
        "source_id": "PM-062.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-des-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth simply connected complex projective contact Fano manifold with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(X)=\\mathbb Z[L]$. Assume that a family of free contact lines covers a dense open subset of $X$. Let $p:Y\\to Z$ be dominant, where $Y$ is a proper integral algebraic space over $\\mathbb C$. Suppose that there are regular sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(T_X\\boxtimes L)\\bigr),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(L\\boxtimes T_X)\\bigr)$$ with the same contact value $$\\label{des:common-value}\n\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n    =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\\in H^0(Y,p^*M).$$ Then there is a unique section $B\\in H^0(Z,M)$ such that $p^*B=\\xi$.",
          "source_id": "PM-062.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $X$ be a smooth simply connected complex projective contact Fano manifold with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(X)=\\mathbb Z[L]$. Assume that a family of free contact lines covers a dense open subset of $X$. Let $p:Y\\to Z$ be dominant, where $Y$ is a proper integral algebraic space over $\\mathbb C$. Suppose that there are regular sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(T_X\\boxtimes L)\\bigr),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(L\\boxtimes T_X)\\bigr)$$ with the same contact value $$\\label{des:common-value}\n\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n    =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\\in H^0(Y,p^*M).$$ Then there is a unique section $B\\in H^0(Z,M)$ such that $p^*B=\\xi$.",

        "exact_quote": "Apply Proposition <a href=\"#des:descent\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"des:descent\">22</a> to the space and tensors of Proposition <a href=\"#ext:moving-pair\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ext:moving-pair\">17</a>. The resulting section $B\\in H^0(X\\times X,L\\boxtimes L)$ is symmetric under exchange of the factors, using the canonical exchange of the two line factors. For the full neighborhood $\\mathcal H$ of $g$ in the space of parametrized degree-two maps provided there, one has $$\\label{des:family-identity}\nB\\bigl(h(z),h(w)\\bigr)\n=(z-w)^2\\,\\theta\\bigl(dh(\\partial_z)\\bigr)\n       \\otimes\\theta\\bigl(dh(\\partial_w)\\bigr),\n\\qquad h\\in\\mathcal H.$$ This identity holds for all pairs of source points, including the diagonal; its displayed expression uses any common affine coordinate.",

        "anchor": "stmt-jet-value-velocity",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the parameter $0\\in\\mathbb P^1$ and its affine coordinate. Its image is open in $T_X$. Its projection is a nonempty open subset $U\\subset X$, and for each $x\\in U$ the velocities of maps in $\\mathcal U$ with $h(0)=x$ form a nonempty open subset of $T_xX$.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Contact structure** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *contact structure* on $X$ is an exact sequence of algebraic vector bundles $$\label{intro:contact-sequence} 0\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow T_X \xrightarrow{\ \theta\ }L\longrightarrow0,$$ with $L$ a line bundle, such that the Levi pairing $$F\otimes F\longrightarrow L, \qquad (v,w)\longmapsto\theta([v,w])$$ is everywhere nondegenerate.
- **Contact Fano manifold** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We call $(X,F)$ a *contact Fano manifold* if $-K_X$ is ample.
- **Adjoint variety** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a simple complex Lie algebra $\mathfrak g$, let $\mathcal O_{\min}(\mathfrak g)$ be its nonzero nilpotent adjoint orbit of smallest dimension, and put $$Y_{\mathfrak g}=\mathbb P(\mathcal O_{\min}(\mathfrak g)).$$
- **Contact degree** ([source](#pm-h-4)): The *contact degree* of a map $f:\mathbb P^1\to X$ is $\deg f^*L$.
- **Contact line and conic** ([source](#pm-h-4)): We call such a map a *contact line* if its contact degree is one and a *conic* if its contact degree is two; these terms impose no embedding assumption.
- **Quaternionic-Kähler metric** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Such a metric is called *quaternionic-Kähler*; *positive* here means that its scalar curvature is positive.
- **Wolf spaces** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The compact symmetric positive quaternionic-Kähler spaces are the *Wolf spaces*.
- **General locus** ([source](#pm-h-5)): “General” means outside a proper Zariski-closed subset unless an analytic open set is explicitly specified.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-red-alternatives) / [proof](#proof-stmt-red-alternatives): Picard reduction and explicit model identification -> Primitive Picard case or an explicit transitive contact model
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lines-suitable-pair) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lines-suitable-pair): Legendrian cone addition and fixed-value map deformations -> Suitable contact-line pair
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-sm-very-free) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sm-very-free): Fixed-end obstruction and moving-end smoothing -> Very free conic and full parametrized neighborhood
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-ext-moving-pair) / [proof](#ext:section): Unmarked compactification, contact-order cancellation and normal extension -> Proper moving-pair space with both regular vector lifts
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-des-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-des-descent): Contact-line tests, split sheets, branch purity and simple connectivity -> Unique descended contact value
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-jet-diagonal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-diagonal): Value–velocity variation and intrinsic continuation of diagonal symbols -> Global quadratic diagonal symbol
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-jet-criterion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-criterion): Symmetric Taylor calculation, immersion and contact Hamiltonians -> First-jet generation and contact transitivity
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-hom-adjoint) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hom-adjoint): Effective algebraic action and highest-weight contact moment map -> Simple adjoint contact variety
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-quaternionic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-jet-good-curves-4): Positive twistor construction and normalized metric uniqueness -> Homothety to a symmetric Wolf space

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.roadmap.v2 -->

{

          "anchor": "stmt-lines-suitable-pair",
          "exact_quote": "There exist a point $x\\in X$ and free parametrized contact lines $f_1,f_2:\\mathbb P^1\\to X$, with $f_1(0)=f_2(0)=x$, such that, for affine parameters at $0$ and $a=f'_1(0)$, $b=f'_2(0)$, $$\\label{lines:pair}\nd\\alpha_x(a,b)\\neq0,\\qquad\nP_{f_1}|_0+P_{f_2}|_0=F_x.$$ In particular the two positive subspaces are complementary Lagrangian subspaces of $F_x$.",
          "source_id": "PM-062.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The geometric existence and global hypotheses are not supplied as finite encoded inputs with an extraction procedure.",
      "step_id": "R2"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-sm-very-free",
          "exact_quote": "There is a map $g:\\mathbb P^1\\to X$ with $\\deg g^*L=2$ and $$\\label{sm:positive-splitting}\ng^*T_X\\simeq\\mathcal O(2)\\oplus\\mathcal O(1)^{\\oplus2n}.$$ The full scheme $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_2(\\mathbb P^1,X)$ of degree-two maps is smooth at $g$, and evaluation at two fixed distinct parameters is submersive there. Its irreducible component through $g$ therefore dominates $X\\times X$ by two-point evaluation. Moreover $g$ is an immersion, and strict positivity of the tangent splitting persists on an open neighborhood of $g$.",
          "source_id": "PM-062.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The geometric existence and global hypotheses are not supplied as finite encoded inputs with an extraction procedure.",
      "step_id": "R3"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ext-moving-pair",
          "exact_quote": "There are a proper integral algebraic space $Y$, a dominant morphism $p:Y\\to X\\times X$, and sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{12}),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{21})$$ with common contact value $$\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n     =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\n\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal M_X).$$ There is an involution $\\iota$ of $Y$ over the interchange of the two factors of $X\\times X$, under which $\\xi$ is invariant using the canonical interchange of the line factors.*\n\n\n\n*Moreover, for a full open neighborhood $\\mathcal H$ of $g$ in $\\operatorname{Hom}_2(\\mathbb P^1,X)$ there is a morphism $$j:\\mathcal H\\times\\mathbb P^1\\times\\mathbb P^1\\longrightarrow Y,\n\\qquad p(j(h,z,w))=(h(z),h(w)).$$ In an affine coordinate containing $z,w$, its pullbacks satisfy $$\\label{ext:hom-vectors}\n\\begin{aligned}\nj^*W_{12}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2h'(z)\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)),\\\\\nj^*W_{21}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes h'(w),\n\\end{aligned}$$ and hence $$\\label{ext:hom-identity}\nj^*\\xi(h,z,w)\n  =(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)).$$ These formulas are independent of the coordinate.",
          "source_id": "PM-062.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The geometric existence and global hypotheses are not supplied as finite encoded inputs with an extraction procedure.",
      "step_id": "R4"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-des-descent",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth simply connected complex projective contact Fano manifold with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Pic}}(X)=\\mathbb Z[L]$. Assume that a family of free contact lines covers a dense open subset of $X$. Let $p:Y\\to Z$ be dominant, where $Y$ is a proper integral algebraic space over $\\mathbb C$. Suppose that there are regular sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(T_X\\boxtimes L)\\bigr),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(L\\boxtimes T_X)\\bigr)$$ with the same contact value $$\\label{des:common-value}\n\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n    =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\\in H^0(Y,p^*M).$$ Then there is a unique section $B\\in H^0(Z,M)$ such that $p^*B=\\xi$.",
          "source_id": "PM-062.1"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The geometric existence and global hypotheses are not supplied as finite encoded inputs with an extraction procedure.",
      "step_id": "R5"
    },
    {
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-jet-criterion",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a connected compact complex contact manifold with contact quotient $\\theta:T_X\\to L$, and let $\\mathcal I_\\Delta$ be the ideal sheaf of the diagonal in $X\\times X$.       "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-red-alternatives",
          "exact_quote": "Either $\\operatorname{Pic}(X)=\\mathbb Z[L]$, or $(X,F)$ is contact-isomorphic to one of the following standard contact manifolds:*\n\n1.  *the incidence variety $$I=\\{(\\ell,H)\\in\\mathbb P^{n+1}\\times(\\mathbb P^{n+1})^*: \\ell\\subset H\\},\n    \\qquad L=\\mathcal O_I(1,1);$$*\n\n2.  *projective space $\\mathbb P^{2n+1}$ with the contact structure induced by a nondegenerate alternating form on $\\mathbb C^{2n+2}$, and $L=\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb P^{2n+1}}(2)$.*\n\n*In both listed cases a connected algebraic group acts transitively and preserves the given distribution $F$.",

          "exact_quote": "There exist a point $x\\in X$ and free parametrized contact lines $f_1,f_2:\\mathbb P^1\\to X$, with $f_1(0)=f_2(0)=x$, such that, for affine parameters at $0$ and $a=f'_1(0)$, $b=f'_2(0)$, $$\\label{lines:pair}\nd\\alpha_x(a,b)\\neq0,\\qquad\nP_{f_1}|_0+P_{f_2}|_0=F_x.$$ In particular the two positive subspaces are complementary Lagrangian subspaces of $F_x$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-sm-very-free",
          "exact_quote": "There is a map $g:\\mathbb P^1\\to X$ with $\\deg g^*L=2$ and $$\\label{sm:positive-splitting}\ng^*T_X\\simeq\\mathcal O(2)\\oplus\\mathcal O(1)^{\\oplus2n}.$$ The full scheme $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_2(\\mathbb P^1,X)$ of degree-two maps is smooth at $g$, and evaluation at two fixed distinct parameters is submersive there. Its irreducible component through $g$ therefore dominates $X\\times X$ by two-point evaluation. Moreover $g$ is an immersion, and strict positivity of the tangent splitting persists on an open neighborhood of $g$.",

          "anchor": "stmt-ext-moving-pair",
          "exact_quote": "There are a proper integral algebraic space $Y$, a dominant morphism $p:Y\\to X\\times X$, and sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{12}),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal E_{21})$$ with common contact value $$\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n     =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\n\\in H^0(Y,p^*\\mathcal M_X).$$ There is an involution $\\iota$ of $Y$ over the interchange of the two factors of $X\\times X$, under which $\\xi$ is invariant using the canonical interchange of the line factors.*\n\n\n\n*Moreover, for a full open neighborhood $\\mathcal H$ of $g$ in $\\operatorname{Hom}_2(\\mathbb P^1,X)$ there is a morphism $$j:\\mathcal H\\times\\mathbb P^1\\times\\mathbb P^1\\longrightarrow Y,\n\\qquad p(j(h,z,w))=(h(z),h(w)).$$ In an affine coordinate containing $z,w$, its pullbacks satisfy $$\\label{ext:hom-vectors}\n\\begin{aligned}\nj^*W_{12}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2h'(z)\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)),\\\\\nj^*W_{21}(h,z,w)\n  &=(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes h'(w),\n\\end{aligned}$$ and hence $$\\label{ext:hom-identity}\nj^*\\xi(h,z,w)\n  =(z-w)^2\\theta(h'(z))\\otimes\\theta(h'(w)).$$ These formulas are independent of the coordinate.",

Suppose that there are regular sections $$W_{12}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(T_X\\boxtimes L)\\bigr),\\qquad\nW_{21}\\in H^0\\bigl(Y,p^*(L\\boxtimes T_X)\\bigr)$$ with the same contact value $$\\label{des:common-value}\n\\xi=(\\theta\\boxtimes 1)(W_{12})\n    =(1\\boxtimes\\theta)(W_{21})\\in H^0(Y,p^*M).$$ Then there is a unique section $B\\in H^0(Z,M)$ such that $p^*B=\\xi$.",

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-contact-classification**: Every manifold satisfying the contact Fano hypotheses is algebraically contact-isomorphic to the adjoint variety of a simple complex Lie algebra; the given distribution and its quotient line bundle are identified.
- **I-quaternionic-classification**: Under the stated Riemannian hypotheses, the curvature is parallel and the metric is homothetic to a compact symmetric positive quaternionic-Kähler Wolf space.
- **I-symmetric-jets**: A symmetric section with the stated diagonal vanishing and second symbol generates ordinary first jets everywhere; global contact fields span every tangent space and the identity component acts transitively.
- **I-unmarked-boundary**: The stable source is smooth or a nodal pair of rational components of contact degree one; contracted components are excluded.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-distribution**: Treat the projectivized cotangent-bundle alternative as identifying the given contact distribution. -> The asserted biholomorphism identifies underlying manifolds only.
- **A-quaternionic**: Apply Wolf-space classification to a metric with only positive scalar curvature. -> Scalar positivity alone supplies neither the required holonomy containment nor the global manifold hypotheses.
- **A-jets**: Infer contact transitivity from an arbitrary symmetric section. -> Symmetry alone does not supply the diagonal symbol that detects contact and Levi directions.
- **A-generic-fiber**: Use normality of nodal-curve products when smoothness is known only on part of a generic fiber. -> The normality statement requires smoothness and geometric integrality of the entire generic fiber.
- **A-primitive**: Use the immersed conic conclusion without the primitive Picard restriction. -> This conclusion is stated in the primitive Picard case.
- **A-stability-marks**: Transfer the unmarked boundary classification to sources with arbitrary stability markings. -> Markings change the stability condition for contracted components.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The classification and symmetric-jet criterion concern global complex manifolds, geometric existence and holomorphic sections. The source supplies no finite input representation or terminating procedure that constructs the adjoint isomorphism, the descended section or the Wolf-space metric from such inputs. Local jet identities and the unmarked boundary classification do not decide these global hypotheses or conclusions.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The contact-Fano classification assumes a smooth connected complex projective contact Fano manifold with its given contact distribution. Blocked use: ['Applying the contact-Fano theorem to singular, disconnected, nonprojective or non-Fano contact spaces without another supporting result.']
- **L2** (edge_case): The projectivized cotangent-bundle alternative identifies the underlying manifold without identifying the given contact distribution. Blocked use: ['Treating the underlying biholomorphism as a contact isomorphism for the given distribution.']
- **L3** (hypothesis): The quaternionic-Kähler conclusion assumes closedness, connectedness, smoothness, positive scalar curvature and the specified holonomy representation. Blocked use: ['Extending the conclusion to noncompact manifolds, nonpositive scalar curvature or a different holonomy representation.']
- **L4** (hypothesis): The rational-curve conclusion through every point is stated in the primitive Picard case. Blocked use: ['Claiming that this curve conclusion holds without the primitive Picard hypothesis.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): The normality assertion requires the entire generic fibers to be smooth and geometrically integral. Blocked use: ['Replacing the entire-generic-fiber hypothesis by generic smoothness alone.']
- **L6** (edge_case): The boundary source description concerns unmarked stable maps; variable evaluation points do not add stability markings. Blocked use: ['Applying the same boundary description to marked stable maps with additional contracted components.']
- **L7** (hypothesis): Contact-value descent requires the primitive Picard hypothesis, simple connectedness and a covering family of free contact lines. Blocked use: ['Descent from a proper family alone without the free-line and contact-vector-lift hypotheses.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): The symmetric-jet criterion requires compactness and a symmetric global section with the prescribed diagonal vanishing and quadratic symbol. Blocked use: ['Inferring contact transitivity from a local contact form or an arbitrary symmetric section alone.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): Adjoint identification assumes an effective transitive connected algebraic action preserving the contact distribution and an ample contact line. Blocked use: ['Applying the adjoint identification to a non-effective action or an action that does not preserve the contact distribution.']
- **L10** (edge_case): Metric recovery gives the existence of a base isometry; the initially supplied biholomorphism need not preserve the chosen twistor fibrations. Blocked use: ['Assuming that the initially given complex isomorphism itself descends to the recovered base isometry.']
- **L11** (nonconstructive): The smoothing argument chooses a map in a nonempty smooth-source neighborhood without giving a finite input representation or an extraction algorithm. Blocked use: ['Treating existence of a very free conic as an executable construction from arbitrary manifold input.']
- **L12** (unknown): A finite engineering input representation and an executable global classification algorithm are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Claiming a numerical or software implementation of the global classification from this paper alone.']

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-projective-contact",
          "quote": "Let $(X,F)$ be a smooth connected complex projective contact manifold of dimension $2n+1$, where $n\\geq1$. Its second Betti number determines the following alternatives.*\n\n1.  *If $b_2(X)=1$, then $(X,F)$ is contact-isomorphic to the adjoint variety $Y_{\\mathfrak g}$, with its canonical contact distribution, for a simple complex Lie algebra $\\mathfrak g$.*\n\n2.  *If $b_2(X)\\geq2$, then there is a smooth connected complex projective manifold $Z$ of dimension $n+1$ and a biholomorphism $$X\\simeq\\mathbb P(T^*Z).$$ Here $\\mathbb P(T^*Z)$ parametrizes lines in cotangent spaces, equivalently hyperplanes in tangent spaces. This assertion identifies the underlying manifolds, without imposing compatibility with the given distribution $F$.*\n\n*Conversely, every adjoint variety of complex dimension $2n+1$ and every projectivized cotangent bundle in the second alternative admits a contact structure. Thus these are precisely the underlying manifolds of smooth connected complex projective contact manifolds in these dimensions.",

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We resolve the contact-Fano homogeneity conjecture and the Riemannian LeBrun–Salamon conjecture positively. Every smooth connected complex projective contact Fano manifold of complex dimension at least three, with its given contact distribution, is contact-isomorphic to the adjoint variety of a simple complex Lie algebra. Consequently, every closed connected smooth positive quaternionic-Kähler manifold of real dimension $`4m\geq8`$ is homothetic to a compact symmetric Wolf space.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Let $X$ be a smooth connected complex projective variety of dimension $2n+1$, where $n\geq1$. A *contact structure* on $X$ is an exact sequence of algebraic vector bundles <a id="intro:contact-sequence"></a>$$\label{intro:contact-sequence}
 0\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow T_X
 \xrightarrow{\ \theta\ }L\longrightarrow0,$$ with $L$ a line bundle, such that the Levi pairing $$F\otimes F\longrightarrow L,
 \qquad (v,w)\longmapsto\theta([v,w])$$ is everywhere nondegenerate. In a local frame of $L$, write $\alpha$ for the scalar one-form representing $\theta$. The condition is equivalent to $\alpha\wedge(d\alpha)^n$ being nowhere zero. We call $(X,F)$ a *contact Fano manifold* if $-K_X$ is ample.

For a simple complex Lie algebra $\mathfrak g$, let $\mathcal O_{\min}(\mathfrak g)$ be its nonzero nilpotent adjoint orbit of smallest dimension, and put $$Y_{\mathfrak g}=\mathbb P(\mathcal O_{\min}(\mathfrak g)).$$ This is the unique closed adjoint orbit in $\mathbb P(\mathfrak g)$. Its canonical contact structure is obtained by projectivizing the Kirillov–Kostant–Souriau symplectic cone.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $(X,F)$ be a smooth connected complex projective contact Fano manifold of dimension $2n+1$, with $n\geq1$, and let $L=T_X/F$. There exist a finite-dimensional simple complex Lie algebra $\mathfrak g$ and an algebraic isomorphism $$\varphi:X\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }Y_{\mathfrak g}$$ whose differential carries the given distribution $F$ to the canonical contact distribution on $Y_{\mathfrak g}$. Moreover, $$L\simeq\varphi^*\bigl(\mathcal O_{\mathbb P(\mathfrak g)}(1)|_{Y_{\mathfrak g}}\bigr).$$*

</div>

Throughout the paper, $\mathbb P(E)$ parametrizes lines in $E$, with tautological subbundle $\mathcal O_{\mathbb P(E)}(-1)$. The two models separated by the Picard reduction are $$\begin{array}{c|c|c}
 X&L&\mathfrak g\\ \hline
 \mathbb P^{2n+1}&\mathcal O(2)&\mathfrak{sp}_{2n+2}(\mathbb C)\\[2pt]
 \mathbb P(T^*\mathbb P^{n+1})&\mathcal O(1,1)|_X&\mathfrak{sl}_{n+2}(\mathbb C).
 \end{array}$$ In the second row, $X$ is the incidence divisor of points and hyperplanes in $\mathbb P^{n+1}\times(\mathbb P^{n+1})^*$. In the first, the adjoint embedding is the quadratic Veronese embedding, so its hyperplane bundle is $\mathcal O(2)$. The proof then treats the remaining case $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)=\mathbb Z[L]$.

Two consequences will be proved after the classification in Section <a href="#hom:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hom:section">8</a>.

<a id="stmt-thm-contact-fano-ke"></a>
<div id="thm:contact-fano-ke" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Kähler–Einstein existence). *Let $(X,F)$ be a smooth connected complex projective contact Fano manifold of complex dimension $2n+1$, where $n\geq1$. Then $X$ admits a Kähler–Einstein metric of positive scalar curvature for its given complex structure.*

</div>

For projective contact manifolds without a Fano hypothesis, the results of Demailly and Kebekus–Peternell–Sommese–Wiśniewski provide a reduction to which Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> applies.

<a id="stmt-thm-projective-contact"></a>
<div id="thm:projective-contact" class="corollary">

**Corollary 3** (Projective contact manifolds). *Let $(X,F)$ be a smooth connected complex projective contact manifold of dimension $2n+1$, where $n\geq1$. Its second Betti number determines the following alternatives.*

1.  *If $b_2(X)=1$, then $(X,F)$ is contact-isomorphic to the adjoint variety $Y_{\mathfrak g}$, with its canonical contact distribution, for a simple complex Lie algebra $\mathfrak g$.*

2.  *If $b_2(X)\geq2$, then there is a smooth connected complex projective manifold $Z$ of dimension $n+1$ and a biholomorphism $$X\simeq\mathbb P(T^*Z).$$ Here $\mathbb P(T^*Z)$ parametrizes lines in cotangent spaces, equivalently hyperplanes in tangent spaces. This assertion identifies the underlying manifolds, without imposing compatibility with the given distribution $F$.*

*Conversely, every adjoint variety of complex dimension $2n+1$ and every projectivized cotangent bundle in the second alternative admits a contact structure. Thus these are precisely the underlying manifolds of smooth connected complex projective contact manifolds in these dimensions.*

</div>

The Riemannian application concerns a smooth manifold of real dimension $4m$, $m\geq2$, whose metric has holonomy contained in $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(m)\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(1)$ in its standard representation. Such a metric is called *quaternionic-Kähler*; *positive* here means that its scalar curvature is positive. The compact symmetric positive quaternionic-Kähler spaces are the *Wolf spaces*.

<a id="stmt-thm-quaternionic"></a>
<div id="thm:quaternionic" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4**. *Let $(M^{4m},g)$ be a closed connected smooth Riemannian manifold, where $m\geq2$, with holonomy contained in $\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(m)\mathop{\mathrm{Sp}}(1)$ in the standard representation and with positive scalar curvature. Then $$\nabla\mathop{\mathrm{Rm}}_g=0.$$ Moreover, $(M,g)$ is homothetic to a compact symmetric positive quaternionic-Kähler Wolf space.*

</div>

Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> resolves the smooth contact-Fano homogeneity conjecture positively. Corollary <a href="#thm:quaternionic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:quaternionic">4</a> gives the positive resolution of the Riemannian LeBrun–Salamon conjecture in every real dimension $4m$ with $m\geq2$. The link is the positive twistor construction: the twistor space is contact Fano, so the general algebraic theorem applies to it. Simple connectivity of the contact Fano twistor space and its $S^2$-bundle structure show that the original manifold is simply connected. The metric is recovered from the resulting complex twistor space in Section <a href="#tw:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tw:section">9</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and prior work

The conjectural classification has two related settings. Wolf’s work relates compact quaternionic symmetric spaces to homogeneous complex contact spaces <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Wolf1965](#ref-Wolf1965)]; the adjoint-variety description of their twistor fibration is recalled in <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[LandsbergManivel2004](#ref-LandsbergManivel2004), Section 3.9, pp. 105–106]. The twistor construction of Salamon, independently due to Bérard-Bergery, associates a contact Fano manifold to a closed connected positive quaternionic-Kähler manifold <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Salamon1982](#ref-Salamon1982), [LeBrun1995](#ref-LeBrun1995)]. LeBrun’s converse characterizes the contact Fano manifolds arising in this way by the existence of a Kähler–Einstein metric <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[LeBrun1995](#ref-LeBrun1995), Theorem A]. The finiteness and rigidity program of LeBrun and Salamon <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[LeBrun1993](#ref-LeBrun1993), [LeBrunSalamon1994](#ref-LeBrunSalamon1994)] provides the historical setting for the homogeneity conjecture. The general algebraic formulation, stated for example in <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[Buczynski2010](#ref-Buczynski2010), Conjecture 1.1], concerns every contact Fano manifold.

The projective-contact reduction has two ingredients. Kebekus, Peternell, Sommese and Wiśniewski classify projective contact manifolds with non-nef canonical bundle as either Fano with $b_2=1$ or projectivized cotangent bundles <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KPSW2000](#ref-KPSW2000), Theorem 1.1]. Demailly’s integrability theorem excludes pseudoeffectivity of the canonical bundle of a compact Kähler contact manifold; his Corollaries 3–4 give the reduction used in Corollary <a href="#thm:projective-contact" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:projective-contact">3</a> . Within the Fano class, Mori’s ample-tangent theorem and the Kobayashi–Ochiai index theorem identify the incidence and projective-space alternatives <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[Mori1979](#ref-Mori1979), [KO1973](#ref-KO1973)]. In the remaining primitive case, Kebekus establishes the covering contact lines and the spanning of their tangent directions <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Kebekus2001](#ref-Kebekus2001)]. For $n\geq2$, the corrected contact-line theorem gives a smooth, possibly disconnected, Legendrian variety of directions <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[Buczynski2010](#ref-Buczynski2010), corrected version, Theorem 3.1 and Remark 3.2]. The case $n=1$ is treated directly in Section <a href="#lines:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lines:section">3</a>. These results identify the geometric objects from which the smoothing, tensor descent and diagonal arguments below begin.

Earlier low-dimensional classifications were obtained by Poon and Salamon for compact positive quaternionic-Kähler manifolds in real dimension eight <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[PoonSalamon1991](#ref-PoonSalamon1991)], and by Ye and Druel for contact Fano manifolds in complex dimensions three and five, respectively <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Ye1994](#ref-Ye1994), [Druel1998](#ref-Druel1998)]. Several classification results impose additional geometric or group-theoretic hypotheses. Beauville’s contact moment-map approach gives adjoint classification when the contact linear-system map is generically finite and the contact automorphism group is reductive <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[Beauville1998](#ref-Beauville1998), Theorem 0.1]. Yasukura proves adjoint classification for compact connected complex contact manifolds whose contact line bundle defines a holomorphic immersion <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Yasukura2023v12](#ref-Yasukura2023v12), Theorem 1.1]. The first-jet generation obtained below supplies such an immersion; we give the resulting contact moment-map classification directly. Buczyński and Wiśniewski classify the closed positive quaternionic-Kähler manifolds in real dimensions twelve and sixteen, and the contact Fano manifolds in complex dimensions three through nine with reductive automorphism group <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[BW2022](#ref-BW2022), Theorems 1.1–1.2]. Occhetta, Romano, Solá Conde, and Wiśniewski prove the adjoint classification in the primitive Picard case under reductivity and the rank bound $r\geq\max\{2,(n-3)/2\}$ in complex dimension $2n+1$, where $r$ is the rank of the contact automorphism group <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[OcchettaEtAl2021](#ref-OcchettaEtAl2021), Theorem 6.1]. On the Riemannian side, Brendle and Semmelmann establish symmetry for compact positive quaternionic-Kähler manifolds of real dimension at least eight under nonnegative sectional curvature <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BrendleSemmelmann2025](#ref-BrendleSemmelmann2025), Corollary 2].

The use of contact lines and two-line loci has substantial antecedents. Buczyński attributes the study of loci swept out by two contact lines to Wiśniewski <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[Buczynski2010](#ref-Buczynski2010), Introduction] and develops their divisors, duality, and associated bilinear forms <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Buczynski2010](#ref-Buczynski2010), Introduction and Theorem 1.3]. The Legendrian secant geometry of Landsberg and Manivel <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[LandsbergManivel2007](#ref-LandsbergManivel2007), Proposition 17(2) and its proof] is used in Buczyński’s Proposition 3.5, which also records the disjoint-join statement <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Buczynski2010](#ref-Buczynski2010)]. Here a suitable pair of contact lines is smoothed, two vector lifts are extended across the unmarked degree-two boundary, and their common contact value descends to a symmetric tensor on the square. Its diagonal jets generate ordinary first jets of the contact line bundle at every point, which yields transitivity of contact automorphisms. The final jet criterion also applies to compact complex contact manifolds satisfying its tensor hypothesis, without a Fano assumption.

Kobayashi’s preprint claims the Wolf-space classification for complete locally irreducible positive quaternionic-Kähler manifolds of real dimension at least eight <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Kobayashi2008](#ref-Kobayashi2008), Theorem 5.3] and states Kähler–Einstein existence for arbitrary contact Fano manifolds as a separate conjecture <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Kobayashi2008](#ref-Kobayashi2008), Conjecture 1.3]. Corollary <a href="#thm:contact-fano-ke" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:contact-fano-ke">2</a> establishes this existence for the manifolds in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Earlier versions of Yasukura’s preprint asserted unrestricted contact-Fano and quaternionic-Kähler classifications <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Yasukura2009v3](#ref-Yasukura2009v3), Theorem I and Corollary II], <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Yasukura2022v11](#ref-Yasukura2022v11), Theorems C–D and Corollary E]. The author’s later comments identify an error in Theorem 3.2(ii) of the former version <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Yasukura2009v4](#ref-Yasukura2009v4)], and identify Theorem A and Corollary B of the latter version as false <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Yasukura2023v12](#ref-Yasukura2023v12)].

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The mechanism of the proof

The *contact degree* of a map $f:\mathbb P^1\to X$ is $\deg f^*L$. We call such a map a *contact line* if its contact degree is one and a *conic* if its contact degree is two; these terms impose no embedding assumption.

<a id="intro:global-section"></a>
The objective is to produce enough global vector fields preserving $F$ to span every tangent space. After the two explicit Picard alternatives have been handled, we work with $\mathop{\mathrm{Pic}}(X)=\mathbb Z[L]$ and construct a symmetric section $$\label{intro:global-section}
 B\in H^0(X\times X,L\boxtimes L),\qquad
 L\boxtimes L=\operatorname{pr}_1^*L\otimes\operatorname{pr}_2^*L,$$ where $\operatorname{pr}_1,\operatorname{pr}_2$ are the projections from $X\times X$. Near two coincident points the section vanishes to second order, with leading term given by the square of the contact form. This local behavior will force the global contact fields to span. The construction has four stages.

First, at a general point choose two free contact lines, meaning that their pulled-back tangent bundles are globally generated. Their positive-degree tangent-bundle summands span $F_x$ and their tangent vectors have nonzero Levi pairing. Their union can be smoothed while its two outer values move. The key calculation concerns the contrary, fixed-end problem: a first-order smoothing keeping both values fixed would force the Levi pairing to vanish. The fixed-end obstruction space is one-dimensional, so varying the smoothing parameter supplies exactly the missing direction for two-point evaluation. The resulting conic $g:\mathbb P^1\to X$ is *very free*, meaning that every summand of $g^*T_X$ has positive degree (Proposition <a href="#sm:very-free" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:very-free">15</a>). We keep all nearby parametrized maps, not just a chosen one-parameter smoothing, because the later diagonal calculation varies both their values and their velocities.

Second, compactify these conics in the unmarked stable-map space. Its universal-curve square parametrizes a source curve together with two variable evaluation points. On a smooth source, the coordinate-independent tensor $$(z-w)^2\partial_z\otimes\partial_w$$ gives two vector-valued sections, one retaining the tangent vector in the first factor and one retaining it in the second. Applying the contact form to both factors gives their common contact value. The singular unmarked sources have exactly two degree-one components. At a node of thickness $r$, the contact differential vanishes to order at least $r$, canceling each possible pole of the tensor. Both vector-valued sections consequently extend over the proper family (Proposition <a href="#ext:moving-pair" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:moving-pair">17</a>).

Third, the common contact value descends from this proper family to the section $B$ in <a href="#intro:global-section" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="intro:global-section">[intro:global-section]</a>. The two vector lifts test contact lines in the two factors of $X\times X$. On every proper integral component dominating a test line, the relevant lift has constant projection to the trivial quotient of the pulled-back tangent bundle. This forces every generic sheet of the finite contact-value image to split over that line. A test line meeting a hypothetical branch divisor transversely would instead produce a sheet of degree greater than one. Purity and simple connectedness then complete the descent (Proposition <a href="#des:descent" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="des:descent">22</a>).

Finally, on the full neighborhood of $g$ the section satisfies $$B(h(z),h(w))=(z-w)^2\,
 \theta(h'(z))\otimes\theta(h'(w)).$$ The available value and velocity variations make its constant and linear diagonal terms vanish and identify the quadratic symbol with $\theta^2$. Each of these intrinsic identities extends from a nonempty open subset to all of $X$. Symmetry then determines the cubic Taylor term in local coordinates and a line frame. The quadratic and cubic terms imply that global sections of $L$ generate first jets at every point. Contact Hamiltonians therefore span every tangent space, giving contact transitivity (Theorem <a href="#jet:criterion" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="jet:criterion">28</a>). The contact moment map identifies the resulting homogeneous manifold, with its given distribution, with a simple adjoint variety (Proposition <a href="#hom:adjoint" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hom:adjoint">30</a>).

Two parts of this mechanism have independent formulations: contact-value descent from a proper family with the two vector lifts, and the symmetric-jet criterion on a connected compact complex contact manifold. The latter does not require projectivity or Fano positivity. Global diagonal identities are what pass from the initially general points to every point. After transitivity is proved, Corollary <a href="#jet:good-curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="jet:good-curves">32</a> supplies through every point in the primitive Picard case a conic transverse to $F$ with $u^*F\simeq\mathcal O(1)^{\oplus2n}$.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Conventions and organization

All schemes, algebraic spaces and stacks are over $\mathbb C$. Curve families have their scheme-theoretic fibers. “General” means outside a proper Zariski-closed subset unless an analytic open set is explicitly specified. For bundles $E_1,E_2$ on $X$, we similarly write $E_1\boxtimes E_2=\operatorname{pr}_1^*E_1\otimes
\operatorname{pr}_2^*E_2$.

Section <a href="#red:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="red:section">2</a> proves the Picard reduction. Section <a href="#lines:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lines:section">3</a> selects the pair of contact lines, and Section <a href="#sm:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sm:section">4</a> smooths it to a very free conic. Section <a href="#ext:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:section">5</a> extends the two moving-point tensors over the unmarked boundary; Section <a href="#des:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="des:section">6</a> descends their contact value. Section <a href="#jet:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="jet:section">7</a> turns its diagonal jets into contact transitivity, and Section <a href="#hom:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="hom:section">8</a> identifies the adjoint model and completes Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Section <a href="#tw:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="tw:section">9</a> gives the twistor construction and normalized metric recovery proving Corollary <a href="#thm:quaternionic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:quaternionic">4</a>.

<a id="red:section"></a>
### The contact line and the Picard alternatives

<a id="red:determinant"></a>
Let $X$ be a smooth connected complex projective contact Fano manifold of dimension $d=2n+1$, where $n\geq1$, with contact sequence <a id="red:contact"></a>$$\label{red:contact}
0\longrightarrow F\longrightarrow T_X
\xrightarrow{\theta}L\longrightarrow0.$$ In a local frame of $L$ we write $\alpha$ for $\theta$. With the Levi convention in Section <a href="#sec:introduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:introduction">1</a>, local sections $v,w$ of $F$ satisfy $d\alpha(v,w)=-\alpha([v,w])$. The top exterior power of $d\alpha|_F$ is nowhere zero. Taking determinants gives $$\label{red:determinant}
\det F\simeq L^{\otimes n},\qquad
-K_X\simeq L^{\otimes(n+1)}.$$ In particular $L$ is ample.

<a id="stmt-red-simply-connected"></a>
<div id="red:simply-connected" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *The manifold $X$ is simply connected, and its Picard group is a free abelian group of finite rank, canonically isomorphic to $H^2(X,\mathbb Z)$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-red-simply-connected"><summary>Proof of stmt-red-simply-connected</summary>

*Proof.* We recall an argument that applies to every smooth complex Fano manifold. Yau’s prescribed-Ricci-form Theorem <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[Yau1977](#ref-Yau1977), Theorem 1], proved in <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[Yau1978](#ref-Yau1978)], says that a real closed $(1,1)$-form representing $2\pi c_1(X)$ is the Ricci form of a Kähler metric in any prescribed Kähler class. Ampleness of $-K_X$ supplies a positive representative. For the resulting metric, compactness gives a uniform positive lower bound for the Ricci tensor. The Bonnet–Myers Theorem <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Myers1941](#ref-Myers1941)] makes the universal cover $q:\widetilde X\to X$ compact, so $q$ has finite degree $r$.

The cover is holomorphic and unramified, and the lifted metric is Kähler with positive Ricci tensor. In particular $-K_{\widetilde X}=q^*(-K_X)$ is positive. Kodaira’s projectivity Theorem <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Kodaira1954](#ref-Kodaira1954), Section 3, p. 316] therefore makes the compact manifold $\widetilde X$ projective. The graph of $q$ is a closed analytic subvariety of the projective product $\widetilde X\times X$, so GAGA makes $q$ algebraic <a id="pm-cite-32"></a>[[Serre1956](#ref-Serre1956), Proposition 15]. It is proper with finite fibers, hence finite, and its local analytic inverses show that it is étale. Thus both manifolds and their finite covering are algebraic before we apply projective vanishing and Riemann–Roch.

Kodaira vanishing <a id="pm-cite-33"></a>[[Kodaira1953](#ref-Kodaira1953)], applied to $\mathcal O=K\otimes K^{-1}$, gives $H^i(\widetilde X,\mathcal O_{\widetilde X})=H^i(X,\mathcal O_X)=0$ for $i>0$. Both holomorphic Euler characteristics are one. Since $T_{\widetilde X}=q^*T_X$, Hirzebruch–Riemann–Roch <a id="pm-cite-34"></a>[[Hirzebruch1954](#ref-Hirzebruch1954)] gives $$1=\chi(\widetilde X,\mathcal O_{\widetilde X})
 =\int_{\widetilde X}q^*\operatorname{td}(T_X)
 =r\,\chi(X,\mathcal O_X)=r.$$ Thus $X$ is simply connected. This argument prescribes a Ricci form; it does not require the metric to be Kähler–Einstein.

The exponential sequence and the vanishings of $H^1(X,\mathcal O_X)$ and $H^2(X,\mathcal O_X)$ identify the analytic Picard group with $H^2(X,\mathbb Z)$; GAGA <a id="pm-cite-35"></a>[[Serre1956](#ref-Serre1956)] identifies it with the algebraic Picard group. The cohomology group is finitely generated. The universal coefficient sequence has torsion term $\operatorname{Ext}^1(H_1(X,\mathbb Z),\mathbb Z)=0$, since $H_1(X,\mathbb Z)=0$, and identifies $H^2(X,\mathbb Z)$ with a subgroup of a finitely generated free abelian group. It is therefore free. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-red-alternatives"></a>
<div id="red:alternatives" class="proposition">

**Proposition 6**. *Either $\operatorname{Pic}(X)=\mathbb Z[L]$, or $(X,F)$ is contact-isomorphic to one of the following standard contact manifolds:*

1.  *the incidence variety $$I=\{(\ell,H)\in\mathbb P^{n+1}\times(\mathbb P^{n+1})^*: \ell\subset H\},
    \qquad L=\mathcal O_I(1,1);$$*

2.  *projective space $\mathbb P^{2n+1}$ with the contact structure induced by a nondegenerate alternating form on $\mathbb C^{2n+2}$, and $L=\mathcal O_{\mathbb P^{2n+1}}(2)$.*

*In both listed cases a connected algebraic group acts transitively and preserves the given distribution $F$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-red-alternatives"><summary>Proof of stmt-red-alternatives</summary>

*Proof.* Suppose first that $\operatorname{rank}\operatorname{Pic}(X)>1$. Lemma <a href="#red:simply-connected" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="red:simply-connected">5</a> gives $b_2(X)>1$, and $K_X$ is not nef. Theorem 1.1 of Kebekus–Peternell–Sommese–Wiśniewski <a id="pm-cite-36"></a>[[KPSW2000](#ref-KPSW2000)] states that a projective contact manifold with non-nef canonical bundle is either Fano with $b_2=1$, or a projectivized tangent bundle over a smooth projective manifold. Their convention is the projectivization of one-dimensional quotients. In our convention of lines, the second alternative is $$X\simeq\mathbb P(T^*Z),\qquad \dim Z=n+1.$$ For a rank-$r$ bundle $E$ in the lines convention, $$K_{\mathbb P(E)}=\mathcal O_{\mathbb P(E)}(-r)
 \otimes\pi^*(K_Z\otimes(\det E)^{-1}).$$ Taking $E=T^*Z$ cancels the factor pulled back from $Z$, so $-K_X=\mathcal O_{\mathbb P(T^*Z)}(n+1)$. Consequently $\mathcal O_{\mathbb P(T^*Z)}(1)$ is ample. This is precisely ampleness of $T_Z$, as defined by its tautological quotient line bundle. Mori’s Theorem <a id="pm-cite-37"></a>[[Mori1979](#ref-Mori1979)], that a smooth complex projective manifold with ample tangent bundle is projective space, now gives $Z\simeq\mathbb P^{n+1}$. Thus $X\simeq I$.

We verify the contact structure, rather than only the variety. Put $m=n+1\geq2$ and $A=\mathbb P^m\times(\mathbb P^m)^*$. The incidence variety is a smooth divisor of type $(1,1)$, so adjunction gives $-K_I=\mathcal O_I(m,m)$. Equation <a href="#red:determinant" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="red:determinant">[red:determinant]</a> and torsion-freeness of $\operatorname{Pic}(I)$ give $L=\mathcal O_I(1,1)$. Set $E=\Omega_A^1(1,1)|_I$. The restriction sequence is $$0\longrightarrow\Omega_A^1\longrightarrow\Omega_A^1(1,1)
 \longrightarrow E\longrightarrow0.$$ The Euler sequence on $\mathbb P^m$ gives $H^i(\mathbb P^m,\Omega_{\mathbb P^m}^1(1))=0$ for all $i$ and $h^1(\mathbb P^m,\Omega_{\mathbb P^m}^1)=1$. The decomposition of $\Omega_A^1$ and the Künneth formula imply $$H^0(A,\Omega_A^1(1,1))=H^1(A,\Omega_A^1(1,1))=0,
\qquad h^1(A,\Omega_A^1)=2.$$ Hence $h^0(I,E)=2$. The twisted conormal sequence $$0\longrightarrow\mathcal O_I\longrightarrow E
 \longrightarrow\Omega_I^1(1,1)\longrightarrow0$$ and $H^1(I,\mathcal O_I)=0$ then give $h^0(I,\Omega_I^1(1,1))=1$. The tautological contact form on $\mathbb P(T^*\mathbb P^m)$ is a nonzero section of this space. Indeed, at $(\ell,H)$ with $\ell\subset H\subset\mathbb C^{m+1}$, the cotangent line is $\ell\otimes(\mathbb C^{m+1}/H)^*$. Thus the tautological subbundle is $\mathcal O_I(-1,-1)$ and its dual contact line is $\mathcal O_I(1,1)$. The given contact form is a nonzero scalar multiple of the tautological form, and the kernels agree. The simultaneous action of $\operatorname{PGL}_{m+1}$ on points and hyperplanes is transitive on $I$ and preserves this kernel.

Suppose next that $\operatorname{Pic}(X)$ has rank one. Choose its ample generator $A_1$ and write $L=A_1^{\otimes k}$ with $k>0$. If $k\geq2$, the Fano index is $(n+1)k\geq2n+2=d+1$. The Kobayashi–Ochiai index Theorem <a id="pm-cite-38"></a>[[KO1973](#ref-KO1973)] bounds the index of a smooth Fano $d$-fold by $d+1$, with equality only for $\mathbb P^d$. Thus $k=2$, $X\simeq\mathbb P^d$, and $L=\mathcal O(2)$. For $X=\mathbb P(V)$ the Euler sequence identifies $$H^0(X,\Omega_X^1(2))\simeq\bigwedge\nolimits^2 V^*.$$ The twisted one-form corresponding to $\sigma\in\bigwedge^2V^*$ vanishes at $[v]$ if $v$ belongs to the radical of $\sigma$. Our contact quotient is everywhere surjective, so its alternating form has zero radical. All nondegenerate alternating forms on $V$ are equivalent under $\operatorname{GL}(V)$. This identifies the given contact distribution with the standard one, preserved by the transitive connected symplectic group.

The only remaining alternative is $k=1$. ◻

</details>

<a id="red:primitive"></a>
The two listed manifolds are the type $A_{n+1}$ and $C_{n+1}$ adjoint contact varieties. In the projective-space case the adjoint polarization is $\mathcal O(2)$, so its adjoint embedding is the quadratic Veronese embedding. Their contact transitivity also allows them to be included in the uniform homogeneous identification at the end of the proof. In the intervening sections we assume $$\label{red:primitive}
\operatorname{Pic}(X)=\mathbb Z[L].$$

<a id="lines:section"></a>
### Contact lines and a suitable pair

Throughout this section assume <a href="#red:primitive" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="red:primitive">[red:primitive]</a>. A *contact line* is a map $f:\mathbb P^1\to X$ with $f^*L=\mathcal O(1)$, as in Section <a href="#sec:introduction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:introduction">1</a>. Every such map is birational onto its image, since a nontrivial cover would multiply the positive integral $L$-degree. It is horizontal: <a id="lines:horizontal"></a>$$\label{lines:horizontal}
\theta\circ df=0,$$ because $\operatorname{Hom}(\mathcal O(2),\mathcal O(1))=0$.
